# AscendC Knowledge Base — Search Index

> **Workers: read this file FIRST.**
> Search the "Keywords/Aliases" column for your problem, then load the matched file.
> Same concept may have different names — aliases ensure you find it.
>
> **Before any decision (handoff / spawn / done / PARTIAL / skip-verify)**: re-read
> `ANTI_PRESSURE_PROTOCOLS.md` and cite the relevant Px. P1–P8 are LLM-pressure drift
> modes that override technical rules under load.

## Canonical chip naming (always-loaded glossary — resolve any codename here)

> **a5 / 950PR**: `V351` (≈545× in this KB) = **deprecated truncation of `V3510`** = `DAV_3510` = `arch35` / NpuArch 3510 = SocVersion `ASCEND950` = product **Ascend950PR** (prefill; Ascend950DT = decode, same chip). Single-source: `hardware/target/ascend950pr.md` Naming section + cannbot npu-arch §2.5. 950PR sparsity=0 (no 4:2), UB 256KB phys / 248KB usable, BT 4KB.
> **a3 / a2 / 910C**: marketing "910C" = `Ascend910_93` = SocVersion `ASCEND910B` = `DAV_2201` = `arch22` (A2 and A3 share this arch). 910B sparsity=1 (4:2), UB 192KB, BT 1KB.
> SoC `_NNNN` suffix (e.g. `_957b` / `_9589` / `_9382`) = **silicon bin, server-specific** — resolve via `GetSocName()` / `npu-smi`, never hardcode.

## How to Search

1. Identify your task: what op type? what problem? (e.g., "reduction", "alignment", "bf16")
2. Grep this file for keywords: `grep -i "reduction\|reduce" KB_INDEX.md`
3. Load the matched file(s) and read the specific entries

Future cross-backend ports inherit same trap. |

Long-form derivation of Option 1/2/3 from CANN aclnn host-overhead model (30-80µs spec); aclnnGetWorkspaceSize + executor + workspace alloc breakdown; A5 ACLRT_LAUNCH_KERNEL macro internals; why perf_counter+sync of asymmetric callables is methodology fraud (not noise). | When OL-163 anchor isn't enough — load this for the full derivation + CANN-doc citations. |

L1 alternative to OL-145/146 L2 MicroAPI path; SDK header lines 1361/1369/1826/1834 in `dav_3510/kernel_operator_vec_vconv_impl.h` confirm dtype-pair support for fp8_e4m3fn ↔ float and hifloat8 ↔ float. **NOT** supported for fp8_e5m2 / fp4x2 (those still require L2 MicroAPI). | L1/L2 (mx-format-vec-only: LayerNorm, RMSNorm, Softmax, any reduction-class op reading MXFP8) |

## arch22 → arch35 Migration (`port_a3_to_a5` + fp8/mxfp8/mxfp4 + SIMT/L3 + MicroAPI/L2)

> **Trigger this section** when the task involves: arch22→arch35 port, arch35/ kernel, arch35 narrow-float (fp8/mxfp8/mxfp4), Register-based MicroAPI rewrite, SIMT scatter/gather replacement, arch35 CMakeLists.txt build setup, or upstream-reference verification. Entries below are tagged by migration tier (L1 / L2 / L3 / L4) and by sub-domain (cast / sync / scope / build / hardware).

**Decision flow**:

1. Read OL-141 first — inventory upstream `op_kernel/arch35/` as advisory prior art; never skip task-owned generation or independent truth
2. Read OL-143 second — classify the op into L1 / L2 / L3 / L4
3. Load KB references per the tier (table below)
4. Apply EC-/PB- audits at finalize (C41/C42/C43 in aog-self-critic)

### arch22 → arch35 Migration KB Manifest by L-tier

| Tier | Required reading (cite in KB Manifest LOADED) | When |
|---|---|---|
| **L1** (mechanical adapt — every op) | OL-141, OL-142, OL-149, EC-47, EC-48, EC-49, PB-29, PB-30, PB-31 | port_a3_to_a5 with no L2/L3 trigger; OR final-port closure of any tier |
| **L2** (MicroAPI Register-based rewrite) | L1 set + OL-143, OL-146, OL-148 (if bounded-output), OL-152, P-P95, P-P96 | perf-critical OR quant-Cast OR FP8/HiFloat8 OR overflow-mode-needing |
| **L2 + narrow-float (fp8/mxfp8/mxfp4)** | L2 set + OL-144, OL-145, OL-146, OL-147 (if math fns), P-P93, P-P94 | kernel emits or consumes fp8/fp4/hifloat8/e8m0 |
| **L3** (SIMT) | L1 set + OL-143, OL-150, OL-151, OL-152, PB-32 | scatter/gather + simple index logic + large data |
| **L4** (escalate) | OL-143 + escalate to aog-researcher | tiling needs IsRegbaseSocVersion / UB > 40KB beyond SIMT DCache |

| [PLATFORM_BUGS.md#PB-39](target/ascendc/PLATFORM_BUGS.md) | **PB-39 (bisheng --enable-simt V351/A5)** — `select i1` codegen on `dav-c310-vec` fires `Copy register different width` (or segfault in HiTPE DAG→DAG select). Affects code paths emitting `--enable-simt` IR (generated pure-SIMT ports, hand-written pure-SIMT kernels, possibly some compiler-generated SIMT lowering). NOT relevant to ordinary AscendC operator dev that uses SIMD intrinsics + `Select<T>()`. CANN 9.1.0-beta.1 partial-fixes simple patterns; complex kernels still fail. Secondary `MaxThreads out of range!` surfaces after select-i1 is patched. Workaround: 6-step IR-rewrite pipeline (`&&`→`&` in `array.h` asserts + `select i1`→`br/phi` rewrite + brute-force `simt-max-threads` annotation + `-cc1 -x ir -O0` recompile). | --enable-simt mode only; cross-ref OL-71 (`__CCE_AICORE__` arch macros), PB-4 (other bisheng-codegen bug class) |
| [PLATFORM_BUGS.md#PB-40](target/ascendc/PLATFORM_BUGS.md) | **PB-40 (MIX_AIC_1_2 multi-entry V220, non-fatal)** — stdout `RegisterAscendBinary mix ret 107000` + bare `[ERROR]` printed AT TEARDOWN after kernel main entry registered+ran+produced output. Triggered when one `.cpp` has ≥2 `KERNEL_TASK_TYPE_DEFAULT(KERNEL_TYPE_MIX_AIC_1_2)` entry-pairs but only one is invoked per run (unused entry's deferred binary registration fails). Independent of matmul-primitive (manual Mmad vs Matmul-lib both surface it on V220). Distinct from PB-28 (AIV_ONLY + fatal-never-register). Fix candidates (unverified): collapse to single entry per .cpp, OR split entry-pairs into separate .cpp files. | L4 + MIX_AIC mixed-mode emit; cross-ref PB-28 |
| [PLATFORM_BUGS.md#PB-34](target/ascendc/PLATFORM_BUGS.md) | **PB-34 (FA cube+vec V220)** — `MatmulImpl<>` + manual `CrossCoreSetFlag<0x2>/WaitFlag` + `KERNEL_TYPE_MIX_AIC_1_2` is deadlock on V220 (FFTS sync-slot conflict between user-owned flag chain and KFC-internal protocol). Two valid V220 patterns: (A) tile-MMAD primitives + manual CrossCore, OR (B) `MatmulImpl<>` + KFC-implicit sync ONLY (NO manual CrossCore). Pre-build static guard: same kernel file with both `MatmulImpl<` AND `CrossCoreSetFlag<0x2` is the smoking gun. **FULL-OP no-reproduce confirmed on A5 (2026-06-15, GDN chunk_gated_delta_rule): the full multi-stage upstream V220 kernel (8 MatmulImpl<> + MIX_AIC_1_2 + manual CrossCoreSetFlag<0x2>/WaitFlag) compiled first-try + ran + 122/122 T1 on Ascend950PR/CANN-9.1.T500 → for a V220 cube-MIX fused op the DEFAULT A5 route is a LIGHT PORT (keep MatmulImpl<>+flag chain; adapt only ACLRT_LAUNCH entry + host tiling), NOT a regbase rewrite.** | L4 (fused-attention with cube+vec coordination); cross-ref CAND-FA1, EC-68, OL-220 |
| [PLATFORM_BUGS.md#PB-35](target/ascendc/PLATFORM_BUGS.md) | **PB-35 (FA pipe-sync V220 + V351)** — `event_t(0..3)` for cube-internal pipe sync (`MTE1_M` / `M_FIX` / `MTE2_MTE1`) collides with cross-core `CrossCoreSetFlag<0x2>(FLAG_CANON_DONE=0 / FLAG_MM1_DONE=1 / ...)` chain in `KERNEL_TYPE_MIX_AIC_1_2` mode → silent hang at `torch.npu.synchronize()`, no fault thrown. Fix: cube-internal pipe sync MUST use `event_t(N)` with N ≥ 4; reserve IDs 0..3 for cross-core FA-class flag chain. Pre-build grep guard: same kernel file with overlapping IDs in both `CrossCoreSetFlag<0x2>(...)` and `SetFlag<HardEvent::*>(event_t(...))` is the smoking gun. | L4 (FA cube+vec pipe sync); cross-ref PB-34, CAND-FA1 |
| [PLATFORM_BUGS.md#PB-36](target/ascendc/PLATFORM_BUGS.md) | **[ARCHIVED/DEPRECATED 2026-05-22] PB-36 (FA canon-AIV V220)** — Original framing as "V220 hardware DataCopy bug + Python permute workaround" was **wrong**: CANN's own `aclnnFlashAttentionScoreV2` works on the same V220 / CANN 9.0.0 without any workaround. The recommended Python `torch.reshape().permute().contiguous()` was a No-Delegation rule violation. **SUPERSEDED by CAND-FA-CANON-FREE**. Entry preserved for audit trail per safety rule 5. Do NOT cite as a current pattern. | DEPRECATED — see CAND-FA-CANON-FREE |
| [patterns/unverified/candidates.md#CAND-FA-CANON-FREE](target/ascendc/patterns/unverified/candidates.md) | **CAND-FA-CANON-FREE (canon-free FA cube path on V220)** — Eliminate the AIV layout-canonicalize stage entirely. mm1/mm2 use `MatmulImpl::SetOrgShape(orgM=S, orgN=sS, orgKa=sS, orgKb=sS, orgKc=S_or_D)` 5-arg variant where `sS` is the source layout's physical row stride (= H for BSH, = D for BNSD, etc.); matmul does strided GM→L1 loads internally per-(b,n) head offset, matching CANN flash_attention_score's pattern. mm2 output stays BNSD-internal contig (matmul C-side does NOT accept layout stride — empirically tested, 5–38% error if attempted). A separate AIV postprocess stage transforms BNSD→user layout via per-row contig DataCopy (no `srcStride`/`dstStride` parameter used) + explicit `SetFlag/WaitFlag<HardEvent::MTE2_MTE3>` sync. Avoids V220 strided-DataCopy bug class entirely. **Verified 9/10 cube + 9/9 Pass B at max_abs≤6.1e-5; 0.603× CANN perf at B=1,S=64,N=2,D=64,BSH,fp16. Correctness scope (PR #106): `S*D ≤ 8192 AND S*Skv ≤ 8192`** (kw-1-derived algorithm). **PR #107 dropped `S*Skv` constraint via KV-tiling; PR #109 dropped `S*D` constraint via Q-tiling.** Now: arbitrary `S, Skv` modulo `T_q=T_kv=64` divisibility. | L4 (fused-attention; future: any cube-heavy op needing layout-aware GM gather); cross-ref PB-36 (superseded), PB-9/22 (V220 MTE family), PB-34/35 (V220 mixed-mode minefield — reason row-tiled rewrite uses multi-launch) |

Explicit-generic offset-arithmetic: deal→unflatten→reflatten region-base table, reverse-partial pivot `(fwdPartialLen-1)%cores`, TND accum-walk. Index-arithmetic executability empirically confirmed (kw-wb5 partition-oracle PASS 2026-06-03, 0 load-bearing formulas invented). Per-layout output offset `Σ idx*stride + subRowTerm` (PIECE C, generic). **PIECE D = the exact carrier byte-layout (field order/widths/bitfield packing) HITS THE COPY-LINE (C34c>5%, fundamentally-verbatim) — FLAGGED as a hard reproducibility-copy boundary, NOT added.** Conclusion: mechanism+host-structure+offset KB-reproducible; carrier byte-layout must be a single shared header authored once (cannot be reconstructed from prose). | L3 (unverified candidate; any MIX cube+vec op with host scalar-block on cube critical path); cross-ref CAND-FA-COREDIST-1 (orthogonal — consumes the split-mode/prefix fields this entry's host-structure sets), cv_reference_concrete_params §cross_core_sync/§kernel_block_iteration (orthogonal — per-tile ring sync vs this one-shot bootstrap), CAND-OPAQUE-STRUCT-RUNTIME-VERIFY (POD-layout-is-a-contract) |

Operationalizes CLAUDE.md "No PyTorch/CANN Delegation, No CPU Fallback" — prose rule becomes a grep-able test. | L0 (AscendC agent discipline; all supported ops and modes); cross-ref OL-160/167/172/175 (anti-cheat siblings), CLAUDE.md No-Delegation rule, PR #103/#106 worked example |
| [patterns/unverified/candidates.md#CAND-FA-MULTI-LAUNCH-PERF-GAP](target/ascendc/patterns/unverified/candidates.md) | **CAND-FA-MULTI-LAUNCH-PERF-GAP (5-delta structural perf comparison vs CANN single-launch fused FA)** — When multi-launch row-tiled FA shows large perf gap vs CANN (measured 0.025× at S=512, 0.007× at S=1024 on PR #109), 5 specific kernel-internal pipelining choices account for the 100× gap. Each delta has measured CANN code reference + adopt-feasibility + headroom estimate: (1) L0C accumulator residency across KV-tiles (CANN keeps `O[T_q, D]` in L0C; we GM round-trip every iter — 80% mem traffic save, requires single-launch), (2) row-wise online softmax `[s1_vec=8, s2_aligned]` fp32 buffers (~8KB live) vs our full `[T_q, T_kv]*4` ~16KB (12KB UB freed, ~1-2h to adopt within multi-launch), (3) host-side `CalcS1S2BasicBlock` tiling vs our hard-coded 64×64 (2-4× tile area, ~1h), (4) alpha-rescale fused into mm2's vec2 post-process vs our separate `fa_scale_and_accumulate` AIV launch (25-33% launch reduction, ~2-3h, medium risk), (5) KFC-implicit single-kernel event-ordered task pipeline (CANN's third variant — neither Pattern A's user-CrossCoreSetFlag nor Pattern B's REGIST_MATMUL_OBJ; 10× launch reduction, HIGH risk, needs falsification probe + ~6-8h). Optimization roadmap: #2+#3 first (~3h, ~2-3× gain), then #4 (~3h, +1.5×), then #1+#5 together (~8h, +10×). ## §By Symptom — Cross-cut index for performance / behavior diagnoses (V3.7.10, 2026-05-03)

When an op exhibits a SYMPTOM (e.g. "scalar pipe is the bottleneck", "merge bottleneck on chunked top-K", "fp16/bf16 path differs from fp32"), this section maps symptom → MANDATORY KB files to load. Agents (aog-fused-optimizer, aog-kernel-optimizer, aog-precision-probe) MUST grep this section by symptom-tag and add the listed files to their `KB Manifest LOADED` block before producing any verdict.

**Why this section exists**: KB_INDEX above indexes by op-domain (sort, scatter_add, etc.) — sufficient when you know the op type, but doesn't surface architectural-tier optimizations indexed by performance symptom. fo-1 on op#9 (2026-05-03) loaded `patterns/domains/sort.md` (correctly, op was top-K) but missed `OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md` and `hardware/target/ascend950pr.md §Reg-based` (where the actual optimization lever lived for the symptom "aiv_scl_ratio = 0.90 on A5"). Result: declared structural ceiling at 0.385× when the right answer was a primitive-class swap. This index closes that gap.

| Symptom | Required reading (load all + cite in KB Manifest) | Why |
|---------|---------------------------------------------------|-----|
| **scalar-pipe-bound on A5** (`aiv_scl_ratio > 0.3` and `target=a5`) | OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-54 (reg-based SIMD VERIFIED on A5) + patterns/unverified/candidates.md §P-REG-1 + hardware/target/ascend950pr.md §"Reg-based vs Mem-based SIMD 实现" | A5 V351 has reg-based VEC ops that A2/A3 don't. Scalar GetValue/SetValue chains can be replaced with `Reg::Compare + Reg::Select` keeping intermediates in registers. Mem-based = UB load/store every step; reg-based = registers across the whole inner loop. Mandatory check before declaring "scalar-pipe ceiling" on A5. |
| **fused-op merge bottleneck** (Phase 1 chunk-merge dominates per-row dur) | hardware/target/ascend950pr.md §MrgSort + patterns/domains/sort.md §P-P43 + OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-54 | MrgSort vec-pipe primitive (4-queue merge of pre-sorted runs) is the canonical replacement for scalar 2-pointer merge. Combined with reg-based for the inner compare/select. |
| **"structural ceiling" / "no actionable Kind-1 path" on perf** | aog-self-critic C28 (analytical-only structural-ceiling = reward-hacking) + this §By Symptom + the verdict-template in C27 | Before declaring ceiling, MUST cite per-sub-op msprof on the FUSED reference op + standalone sub-op times + adv_api primitive search results. Analytical-only verdicts are blocked. |
| **fused op + optimizer plateau** | hardware/target/ascend950pr.md (FULL file, not just "load it") + OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-54 + ASCENDC_API_CATALOG.md §"adv_api / Reg API" | The agent MUST scan ascend950pr.md for arch-specific primitives. fo-1 on op#9 loaded the file path conceptually but skipped reading §Reg-based — that's the failure mode this entry catches. |
| **multi-step fusion** (cast→mul→reduce→broadcast etc.) | OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-54 + patterns/unverified/candidates.md §P-REG-1 + ASCENDC_LANGUAGE_REFERENCE.md (TPosition tables for reg-resident TPositions) | Reg-based intermediates eliminate the UB round-trip per step. |
| **bf16 perf gap vs fp16/fp32** (different perf profile by dtype) | hardware/target/ascend950pr.md §dtype matrix + patterns/domains/precision.md + OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-65 | bf16 is 7-bit mantissa; some primitives (e.g. MERGE_SORT in adv_api/topk) reject bf16. Dtype matrix tells you what's supported on which path. |
| **edge_dataset Pass A passes but Pass B regresses on a dtype** | OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-83 (case-set instability + family-vs-index floor) + this row + edge_dataset coverage check | If edge_dataset is fp32-only and the kernel is dtype-templated, Pass A 16/16 doesn't validate fp16/bf16 paths. Methodology bug discovered on op#9 kw-5 (2026-05-03) — input_gen.py needs dtype stratification. |
| **vendor reference perf is N× faster (N>2) AND we don't have an algorithmic hypothesis for the gap** | aog-researcher invocation MANDATORY before writing optimization_directive.md + msprof JSON of vendor call (kernel symbol decomposition: `aclnnFooBar_Sort_AiCoreForX` reveals algorithm class) + public adv_api headers grep (which primitives the vendor likely uses) + hiascend.com docs for the op family + (Phase A: WITHOUT reading internal `opp/built-in/op_impl/` source per CLAUDE.md NPUKernelBench rule; Phase B: only with explicit user authorization for one-time CANN-source-read exception) | Discovered op#9 9_TopKTopP 2026-05-03: 24-hour iteration with ko-1/kw-2/kw-3/kw-4/kw-5/pp-2/fo-1/fo-2 spawns produced ZERO aog-researcher invocations even though "CANN is 3.35× faster, why?" was the load-bearing unanswered question for the entire chain. Each agent wrote a directive (directive-route in state machine) which short-circuited the researcher escalation path. **V3.7.11 fix**: state machine now has `await_optimizer → await_researcher` and `await_fused_optimizer → await_researcher` edges that fire when (perf below threshold) AND (`workspace/{op}/cann_strategy_inference.md` absent) AND (relevant log doesn't cite vendor algorithmic strategy). Without researcher consultation, multi-agent perf chase produces "we tried N angles within our current architecture" but never "we know why vendor's architecture is different and whether that's reproducible". The research output `cann_strategy_inference.md` is the missing artifact. |
| **MIX cube+vec silent hang / deadlock** (`507014` / `507015` on a mixed cube+vector kernel) | PLATFORM_BUGS.md §PB-34 + OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-275 + ERROR_CORRECTIONS.md §EC-68 + OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-220 + `patterns/unverified/candidates.md` §CAND-FA1 | Keep two concerns separate. **Sync-base compile path**: the host `rtGetC2cCtrlAddr` → kernel argument → `AscendC::SetSyncBaseAddr` idiom is not emitted by the standalone harness and its host translation unit lacks the required CANN include path; this is DEBT-210(d′), not a proven runtime cause or platform law. **Confirmed V220 cause**: combining `MatmulImpl<>`/KFC with manual `CrossCoreSetFlag<0x2>` under `MIX_AIC_1_2` can collide on FFTS slots (PB-34). Use Pattern B (KFC-implicit sync only) or Pattern A (tile-MMAD plus manual flags), but read PB-35 before Pattern A: on arch35/A5, event-id collisions and a shared MODE2 flag can deadlock. The documented A5 handshake uses MODE4, disjoint `id`/`id+16` flags, and the correct consumer pipe. A compiler-generated Pattern-A V220 witness confirms the host FFTS mechanism and deterministic execution, but does not exercise PB-34's KFC collision. Cube-only `AIC_ONLY` remains an option when no vector epilogue is needed. |
| **`507014` silent-hang on a STANDALONE-PYBIND MIX (cube+vec) matmul::Matmul/KFC kernel launched via `aclrtlaunch_*`** — builds+registers clean, then `torch.npu.synchronize()` hangs, host reports aicore timeout `507014` / `INNER_EXIT=124`, no aicore exception — *failure/hang symptom, not perf* | PLATFORM_BUGS.md §PB-53 (DOUBLE-BOOTSTRAP root cause + fix) + `patterns/unverified/candidates.md` §CAND-KFC-standalone-bootstrap-teardown (the open verdict PB-53 resolves) + OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md §OL-275 (managed-lifecycle KFC bootstrap) + PLATFORM_BUGS.md §PB-34 (the DISTINCT sibling `507014` — user-owned CrossCore flags vs KFC slots) | **DOUBLE-BOOTSTRAP (PB-53)**: the `auto_gen_<op>_kernel` device wrapper ALREADY did the full KFC/workspace bootstrap (`set_ffts_base_addr`→`rtGetC2cCtrlAddr` + `SetSysWorkspaceForce(raw)` + `matmul::clearWorkspace` + `workspace = GetUserWorkspace(raw)` = raw+16MB) BEFORE the user kernel. A kernel that RE-RUNS `SetSysWorkspaceForce`/`GetUserWorkspace` on the already-offset ptr double-applies the 16MB reserve → KFC msg-ring lands in user scratch → AIC↔AIV rendezvous in corrupted GM → hang. FIX: kernel must NOT re-bootstrap (`workspace` arg is already the user region; `GetSysWorkSpacePtr()` is the RAW base for `REGIST_MATMUL_OBJ`); launch via `aclrtlaunch_*` (the wrapper path), NOT raw `<<<>>>` (which bypasses the wrapper → the OPPOSITE missing-bootstrap hang, same `507014` symptom). Meta: "missing" and "done twice" have identical symptoms, opposite fixes — this corrects the reverted "GE-supplies-FFTS-so-hand-rolled-cannot" framing (the wrapper's `set_ffts_base_addr` DOES supply FFTS; the bug was a DUPLICATED bootstrap, not a MISSING descriptor). **DISTINCT from PB-34** (same `507014`, different cause: PB-34 = user-owned `CrossCoreSetFlag<0x2>` colliding with `MatmulImpl<>`'s KFC sync-slots). **V220 / CANN 9.0.0 / standalone-pybind matmul::Matmul path** (soc=Ascend910_9392); A5/arch35 resolves the KFC base differently — untested, do NOT carry. |
| **`507015` AIC fixpipe MPU-invalid on a STANDALONE-PYBIND matmul::Matmul kernel** — `The MPU address access is invalid` / `subErrType:4` / `fixp_error0=0xb0b` on the cube's L0C→GM fixpipe write — *fault symptom, not perf* | PLATFORM_BUGS.md §PB-54 (matmul WORKSPACE-BASE wiring root cause + fix) + PLATFORM_BUGS.md §PB-41 (the workspace-prefix sibling — under-alloc vs wrong-base) + `patterns/unverified/candidates.md` §CAND-FA-A5-KFC-WORKSPACE (A5 workspace-base sibling) | **matmul WORKSPACE-BASE WIRING (PB-54)**: passing `GetSysWorkSpacePtr()` (unset/invalid for a plain `ascendc_library` stub with no op-framework bootstrap) to `REGIST_MATMUL_OBJ` → the matmul lib's fixpipe writes L0C→GM to an MPU-protected/invalid address → `507015`. FIX (NON-MIX only — PARTIAL on MIX): pass an EXPLICITLY-allocated valid device workspace base — `aclrtMalloc(GetWorkspaceSize(), ACL_MEM_MALLOC_HUGE_FIRST)`, a buffer DISTINCT from the output C tensor — exactly as catlass `00_basic_matmul`. Single-variable evidence: `GetSysWorkSpacePtr()` base → 507015, explicit workspace base → gone; POSITIVE CONTROL catlass `00_basic_matmul` "Compare success." on the same host/CANN 9.0.0 (fault NOT environmental, NOT MIX-specific — minimal NON-MIX matmul reproduces it). Ruled out: SoC-label (_9382 vs _9392), tiling, tile size, C-target, workspace-SIZING. **⚠ MIX/KFC caveat (UNSOLVED 2026-07-17)**: on a full MIX op a FULLY-SEPARATE `aclrtMalloc` buffer clears 507015 but then HANGS (RC=124) — the REGIST workspace base must be BOTH valid AND coupled to the framework sys-workspace where the KFC ring lives; a disjoint buffer decouples matmul-compute from the KFC ring → AIC↔AIV stall. Do NOT naively swap in a separate buffer on a MIX op. Complete fix (a valid sub-region WITHIN the sys-workspace) is localized but unsolved. **DISTINCT from PB-41** (host UNDER-alloc under the `auto_gen` wrapper vs WRONG-base in a standalone stub). **V220 / CANN 9.0.0 / standalone-pybind matmul::Matmul path** (soc=Ascend910_9392). |

### How agents MUST use §By Symptom

1. After running msprof / collecting initial diagnostics, identify the dominant symptom from the table above
2. For EACH symptom matched, add ALL listed files to `KB Manifest LOADED` block — read each file at the cited section
3. workflow_critic (V3.7.10+) verifies KB Manifest LOADED contains the symptom-required entries before allowing `STRUCTURAL_CEILING` / `PERF_PLATEAU` / `no actionable path` verdicts to commit. Missing entries → block.
4. If a symptom isn't in the table but seems to fit one of the existing categories, add the symptom row to this index as part of the agent's `knowledge_update.md` proposal.

**Provenance**: created 2026-05-03 after op#9 9_TopKTopP fo-1 → pp-2 → kw-4 → kw-5 chain revealed orchestrator + fused-optimizer were missing reg-based path because the KB indexed it by op-domain (sort) and hardware-spec (ascend950pr.md), but no symptom-keyed pointer surfaced it for "scalar-pipe-bound" diagnostics. User pushback: "knowledge base 完全没有这方面的指引… pipeline 出了问题，导致已有的知识不能被贯彻". Fix: this §By Symptom + workflow_critic enforcement + agent-prompt updates.

| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-196](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **Membase vs Regbase selection + `__simd_vf__` programming entry (doc-distill)** — Membase = whole-`LocalTensor` basic-API chain, every step round-trips UB. Regbase = `__simd_vf__`-wrapped compute over explicit `RegTensor<T>`/`MaskReg`/`AddrReg` registers loaded by `LoadAlign/StoreAlign`, intermediate values stay register-resident across the chain; user tiles by `VL`. Pick Regbase when (a) hot inner loop has back-to-back vector ops on same buffer (softmax/norm body), (b) need fine MaskReg/UnalignReg control, (c) want compiler-opt scoping; pick Membase for bulk streaming + portability + Membase-only archs. Call-graph: `__simd_vf__` can only call `__simd_callee__` + `constexpr aicore`. Sync: same-Reg write-then-read is auto-serialized; different-Reg cross-pipe needs `LocalMemBar<src, dst>`. Scope: Reg API documented on V351x (Ascend950PR/A5); V351x doc (Atlas 200I/500 A2) is silent — default to Membase on V351x. Doc-distilled from 编程指南__编程模型__AI_Core_SIMD编程__SIMD编程__Reg矢量计算编程.md (NODE-14, 2026-05-28). [SIMD, paradigm decision, A5, V351x, port_a3_to_a5 optimization track] | L1 |

| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-199](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **When a generation step's correct value is fixed by an upstream design artifact, ENFORCE it with a deterministic post-emit gate — don't rely on the LLM reproducing it** — advisory prose doesn't make an LLM lowering deterministic (it may verbatim-port a sibling / improvise). | [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-200](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **On a MIX_AIC unit, precision-correctness does NOT imply perf-normality — the performant form software-pipelines cube/vec across staggered taskIds; a serialized cube↔vec schedule is correct but half-idle** — a MIX unit couples 1 cube (AIC) + N vector (AIV) cores; a lockstep `while(HasNext()){cube();vec();}` loop passes precision but leaves each engine idle ~half the time. The perf-normal reference instead `taskId%K`-staggers (Vec `i%4` while Cube `(i-1)%4`). Whitebox check: diff generated `Process()` vs reference stagger — no `i%K` offset between cube/vec = collapsed pipeline. Precision-pass is necessary-not-sufficient for MIX ops; suspect collapsed pipeline before re-tuning tiles. | [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-201](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **A pybind-wrapped custom-op's e2e benchmark wall is NOT apples-to-apples vs a native vendor op — compare kernel-device time, decompose the wall before attributing a gap to the kernel** — benchmark times "ours" = `ModelNew.forward` (pybind marshalling + ACL launch + sync + copy-back) vs "vendor" = native torch_npu op (`npu_fusion_attention`, no pybind); ours carries per-call pybind/wrapper overhead the native op never pays, so a REAL kernel-msprof improvement can be invisible at the wall and the wall ratio under-counts the kernel. Fair = kernel-device msprof A/B or native-integration A/B. Action: also report kernel-device A/B; if wall gap >> kernel gap, decompose wall (host pre/post + launch + pybind + sync) + label harness-artifact vs real customer cost. Anchor: FA-A5 path-B (2026-05-31) kernel −24% but e2e-wall NEUTRAL; BNSD-native wall 105us = kernel 36.6us + ~68us pybind/sync. | [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-207](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-207** — **Establish bug-reproducibility BEFORE building a proxy-investigation — a non-reproducible defect's proxy-refutations are not bug-relevant** — gate any mechanism-hunt on first proving the defect is reproducible under controllable conditions; a non-reproducible defect's proxy-refutations test a stand-in for a ghost; a "fix" for it is unverifiable (no fail-before/succeed-after). | [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-208](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-208** — **A deterministic-test discipline is only as valid as its target — the proxy itself can be refuted; keep the protocol, hold the target falsifiable** — fail-before/succeed-after on the SAME verifier-controlled build (anti-tailored, only positives can be tailored); author self-test fast-loop AND independent-verifier final-stamp are BOTH needed; confound-guard (vary exactly one variable, hold topology); non-degeneracy guard (a control arm must stay healthy); delta-applied check ≠ verdict (confirm the RIGHT artifact's md5 changed — host-tiling change → optiling.so md5 moves, kernel.o unchanged). | [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-209](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-209** — **grep the op's own call-graph before attributing a defect to a subsystem; NO_FIRE ≠ clean; don't revive a grep-refuted root by assertion** — (1) a disasm/binding-screen can be internally valid yet pin a fault to a subsystem the op never invokes — grep the op's OWN source for the subsystem's entry symbols (FA: `grep KfcServer/KfcCommClient/InitSocState op_kernel/` = 0, uses hand-`Mmad` → KFC/DEBT-20 root red-herring); (2) for a rare/non-det fault a NO_FIRE run (even ×32000) is inconclusive ≠ clean, and instrumenting the real kernel only helps if the fault fires; (3) refutation-ledger is authoritative — a fatigued re-cite of a refuted root from a stale entry is a flip-flop, re-ground against the latest verdict. | [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-211](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-211** — **STARS2.0 is the hardware mechanism behind A5 multi-core core-split + cross-core sync** — Ascend950 ships a 2nd-gen hardware scheduler STARS (System Task and Resource Scheduler) that dispatches AIC/AIV/CPU/DVPP/SDMA/UB/CCU. Capacity envelope (whitepaper §4.4): **Group scheduling ≤8 Groups** (per-Group core count software-configurable, by-Die grouping for L2-cache locality affinity); **compute partition** AIC/AIV/SDMA into **≤16 resource pools**; **up to 128K single-bit OR 4096 32-bit multi-bit hardware sync flags** (the `CrossCoreSetFlag` flagId 0–10 API range is a per-Group allocation convention, NOT the HW ceiling); **HSCB** ns-level interference-free dispatch bus (explains low A5 launch overhead); **2048** host→device task streams. Grounds OL-210 (global cross-group flags), P-P102 (WorkspaceQueue sync), OL-190/OL-212 (MIX_AIC_1_2 group barrier). Source: Ascend950 NPU 架构白皮书 2026-06-07 §3/§4.4. | L1 (A5 multi-core / cross-core-sync / MIX cube-vec hardware reference; backend=ascendc) |
| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-212](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-212** — **On V351 (A5), the MIX_AIC_1_2 AIV→AIC cross-core flag AND-reduces across BOTH AIV sub-blocks — an idle sub-block that never SetFlags hangs the AIC forever** — under `KERNEL_TYPE_MIX_AIC_1_2` (1 Cube : 2 Vector, the hardware-native ratio per whitepaper §3: 36 AI subsystems each = 1 Cube + 2 Vector), a `CrossCoreSetFlag<...,0x2>` AIV→AIC AND-reduces across the 2 AIV sub-blocks: the AIC's `CrossCoreWaitFlag` completes ONLY when BOTH sub-blocks Set. An idle/early-return sub-block (e.g. single-column FA fills only sub-0) → group-set never counts → **AIC waits forever → HANG** (timeout, zero output). Mitigation: split producer work by row/tile range so both sub-blocks write disjoint slices then Set. **V351/A5 confirmation of OL-190** (which left `unverified_on: A5`). Anchor: FA-A5 multi-core graybox 2026-06-07 — standalone MIX kernel hung at C=1 until both sub-blocks ran every SIG handshake. Cross-ref OL-190 (V220 instance), OL-206 (RESULT-handshake ladder), OL-210 (global flags), `fa_class/cross_core_sync.md` §3. | L1 (MIX_AIC_1_2 cube↔vec cross-core sync; backend=ascendc) |
| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-217](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-217** — **A non-deterministic reference that RETURNS its RNG state is bit-exact verifiable by capture-and-replay; "non-deterministic" ≠ "unverifiable"** — discriminator: does the op expose/return the random state (seed/offset/RNG handle)? YES → capture the returned state from the ref call, feed it to the candidate, compare vs the SAME ref call → bit-exact (run-to-run variation irrelevant). NO → genuinely unverifiable. Trap (P5 failure-wrapping): proving the ref is non-det ≠ proving unverifiable. Tiling-invariance corollary: if the RNG counter is keyed to ABSOLUTE output coords (not loop/core indices), a differently-tiled candidate still bit-matches by replaying the base counter. Anchor: `npu_fusion_attention` returns `(…,seed,offset,…)`; feed to `hasDrop=true` candidate → max_abs_diff=0.0; FA-A5 3_FusionAttention recovered 33 dropout cases 15/15→48/48. Cross-ref OL-90 (UB-scatter non-det, different class — no math-truth), OL-88, P-P103 §Dropout. | L1 (verification methodology / RNG-driven non-det reference; backend=all) |
| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-218](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-218** — **At a finalize gate that re-measures claimed counts, emit a concrete pass count for EVERY pass the runner can reliably reproduce — minimize N/A claims** — every N/A or single-source claim in verification.json is a fragile point: a one-off transient (shared-lane NPU contention, `None` read in a canonical→fallback chain) on that measurement produces a claim-vs-measurement discrepancy and rolls back a correct archive. Leave the FEWEST fragile/N-A claims → emit concrete counts for everything the gate's runner reproduces, so a transient degrades to 1-of-2 confirmation, not total rollback. Anchor: FA-A5 3_FusionAttention — O5 read pass_a=None (transient) → rollback despite independent 48/48; fix promoted pass_b N/A→15/15 → 2/2 VERIFIED, no kernel edit. | L1 (harness/finalize claim-vs-measure policy; backend=all) |
| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-223](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-223** — **In a Reset-bearing multi-stage MIX cube kernel, drive cube-internal L0 pipe sync with Reset-safe dynamic back-to-back `FetchEventID` fences — NOT persistent `Buffer<>` credits, NOT hardcoded event ids** — a user-owned hand-rolled cube inside a multi-stage MIX kernel that calls `pipe_->Reset()` between stages (frees the GLOBAL event pool — PB-45) must fence every cube-internal pipe (MTE2→MTE1/MTE1→M/M→FIX/FIX→MTE2) with a self-contained back-to-back `FetchEventID` Set+Wait inside the single `Run` (carry NO sync state across Reset). Persistent `Buffer<>` priming credits → HANG; hardcoded `EVENT_ID0` → `507015` trap. Keep the AIC↔AIV `CrossCoreSetFlag` workspace handshake SEPARATE from intra-AIC fences. Anchor: `RbSetWaitFlag<EVT>` (FetchEventID Set+Wait); GDN `chunk_gated_delta_rule` regbase A5/V351 0/122→118/122. (The GDN light-port — MatmulImpl + OL-220 recipe — avoids this; OL-223 is the regbase hand-rolled-cube path.) | [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-224](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-224** — **Manual cube `Mmad` correctness on tail / rectangular tiles — `k` must be the REAL contraction extent (not `kAlign`), and a `realM`→`mStep` override must be gated to the NON-transpose load branch** — two manual-cube tile bugs the library hides: (1) **tail-K** — fractal LOAD may use `kAlign=AlignUp(K,C0)` but `Mmad` `k` MUST be real `K`, else it sums padding rows (silent unless `K<C0` / not C0-multiple, e.g. chunk tail `K=4`); (2) **transpose-A `realM` override** — `if(realM)mStep=ceil(realM/16)` is only valid in the non-transpose branch (mStep M-based); in the transpose-A branch mStep is K-based so the override corrupts the K-fragment count (silent on square `M==K`, corrupts rect `M!=K`; FA never tested transpose-A). Anchor: GDN regbase — tail-K fix 4 cases fs 0.155→1.000, transpose gating resolved 10-case rect cluster, 108/122→118/122. NOTE bug #2 located at shared `regbase_matmul.h` template → audit/fix separately. Cross-ref OL-197 (load side), PB-43 (build≠validation class), PB-45/OL-223. | L4 (user-cube manual Mmad tail/rect correctness) |
| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-225](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-225** — **A scalar-pipe write to GM is not coherent for a subsequent cube/MTE2 (or cross-core) read — stage in UB then `DataCopy` (MTE3) to flush** — a scalar `SetValue`/store straight into a `__gm__` address is NOT guaranteed visible to a downstream cube L1 load or another core, even after the producer block "finishes" and despite ordinary pipe barriers; the cube reads stale/garbage. Coherent path: build in a UB `LocalTensor` (on-chip scalar SetValue is fine), then `DataCopy` UB→GM (MTE3) with an `S_MTE3`/`V_MTE3`→`MTE3_MTE2` fence — the MTE3 store is the GM-visible flush. Anchor: GDN S0 transpose (`init[Dv,Dk]ᵀ → S0[Dk,Dv]`) scalar-stored to GM → cube `qs@S0` read stale; UB-stage + DataCopy fixed it (`gdn.cpp:154-168`). Distinct from OL-128 (V220 VEC pipe → TBuf → MTE3, fixed via `TQue<VECOUT>`). Cross-ref OL-128, OL-94, OL-224. | L4 (cube + scalar-staging); soc=Ascend950PR|
| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-226](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-226** — **Whole-grid `SyncAll` requires all blocks co-resident — cap `blockDim = min(work, physical cores)` and tile extra work into a per-block loop with no-op-guarded slots so every block makes the SAME SyncAll count** — two deadlock modes: (1) over-subscription — `blockDim > cores` → runtime schedules in waves → wave-1 barriers wait on never-co-resident wave-2 blocks; (2) count-mismatch — a per-block work-loop with differing trip counts makes barrier N on one block pair with barrier N+1 on another. Fix: `gridDim = min(items, CORES)`; loop `ceil(items/grid)` iters per block; `active = (item < items)` guards each compute step but NEVER skips the loop iteration or the SyncAll. Anchor: GDN one-head-per-block, `Nv≥32 > cores` → `blockDim=Nv` deadlocked; head-tiling (`gdn.cpp:46-52,70-76`) capped grid + equal-count no-op-guarded slots fixed it. Distinct from OL-213 (AiCore-only independent-data: AVOID SyncAll, use `PipeBarrier<PIPE_ALL>`); this is for kernels that LEGITIMATELY need the whole-grid barrier. Cross-ref OL-213, OL-214, OL-216. | L4 (whole-grid SyncAll barrier sizing); soc=Ascend950PR|

Resolves `CAND-KFC-standalone-bootstrap-teardown` (the "op-framework required" verdict was the wrapper-does-it half; fix = don't do it twice) and corrects the reverted "GE-supplies-FFTS-so-hand-rolled-cannot" framing. Meta-lesson: "missing" and "done twice" have identical symptoms, opposite fixes. DISTINCT from PB-34 (that is user-owned CrossCore flags colliding with KFC sync-slots — same `507014`, different cause). Method: list each UB buffer's live range (which phases read/write, down to which row), find slots that are "dead past phase N", reuse for scratch in later phases. **Key: must be dead across phase boundary; buffers still alive within the same phase cannot be aliased**. op#11 evidence: aog-kernel-optimizer Opt4 wrongly picked tmpBuf (still alive) → silent overwrite; aog-fused-optimizer C4 correctly picked otherBuf (dead after SwiGLU) → PASS on first try + 20 KB freed. Watch out for cross-row V↔MTE2 hazard (see PB-17).) | L1 |

Eliminates ~25 GM tiling-reads per call AND the host-side `torch::empty(200B) + .copy_()` H2D (~5–10 µs). Determinism by-construction (single-AIC, no atomicAdd, IterateAll<sync=true>+End()). Validated on op#1 BatchMatmul (Opt0 0.515× → Opt2 1.27×). Carries cleanly to TransA/TransB/BothTrans. See OL-91 / EC-39 / EC-40 for the build invariants.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P69](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P69** — **Cube transposed-input via runtime `SetTensor*(_, isTrans=true)` bool — NOT template ISTRANS** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: Level-3 cube op with one or both operands logically transposed (TransA / TransB / BothTrans, Conv backward weights, Linear with weight transpose). All `MatmulType<...>` template ISTRANS args stay default `false`; the actual transpose driver is the runtime member set by `SetTensorA(gm, /*isTransposeA=*/true)` (or B-side analogue). Tiling field map is shape-flat: M=output_rows, N=output_cols, Ka=Kb=reduction, regardless of which operand transposes. A-side and B-side bools are independent and symmetric — BothTrans = both bools true with no further changes. Validated across the {none, A, B, both} 4-corner lattice on op#1/#4/#5/#3 (each ≤1 precision iter, last two ops 0+0). Direct extension of P-P68. **Anti-pattern**: `MatmulType<...,ISTRANS=true>` + `SetTensorA(_, false)` compiles but produces garbage output (the static template flag is MX-FP8-only, never consulted in ND→ND path). See OL-92 for full mechanism trace.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P70](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P70** — **Fused dequant→activation→quant pipeline algorithm shape (generic for L2 fused activation+quant ops)** (domain: memory_access; trigger: Generic algorithm structure for fused L2 ops of class `dequant→activation→quant`. Members: op#10 SwigluQuant, op#11 DequantSwigluQuant, future GroupNormSiluQuant / RMSnormGeluQuant / etc. **Pipeline**: (1) Dequant: `Cast(x_fp, x_int32, NONE)` then `Mul(x_fp, x_fp, weight_scale)` then `Mul(x_fp, x_fp, activation_scale)` (broadcasted per-row); add bias if non-None. (2) Activation chunk + compute: see P-P71 for layout choice. (3) Smooth-quant: `Mul(out, out, quant_scale)` if quant_scale non-None. (4) Dynamic int8 quant tail: see P-P72. **UB layout for fused ops**: 4-6 buffers per row (x_int32 source, x_fp working, gate, linear, out_fp32, out_int8). For V220 (UB=192KB nominal, ~126KB effective per KC-2 candidate after runtime reservations) tile rows by D. **Per-row round-robin across AIV** — single-AIV-per-row, no inter-AIV reduction needed (quant amax is per-row). Determinism by-construction. Combine with P-P65 (cross-phase buffer aliasing) for tight UB budgets.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P71](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P71** — **Activation-layout convention disambiguation via CANN op-parameter default fingerprinting** (domain: precision; trigger: When a CANN fused op exposes scalar parameters with **non-trivial default values** (irrational constants, small-int saturation limits, named bias terms), those defaults are usually the FINGERPRINT of a published public formula — and CANN is faithfully implementing that formula INCLUDING its data-layout convention. **Workflow**: (1) grep the CANN op definition / `torch_npu.npu_<X>` signature for default values; (2) WebFetch known public OSS reference repos (`github.com/openai/gpt-oss`, `github.com/meta-llama/llama`, `github.com/mistralai/mistral-src`) for the default-value triple; (3) if a match is found, the layout (chunked vs interleaved-stride-2 vs others) matches that source's tensor packing. **Canonical instance** (current evidence): SwiGLU has two valid formulations — (A) **chunked halves** (PyTorch native): `gate, linear = chunk(x, 2, dim=-1)`, used by `swiglu_mode=0` of `aclnnDequantSwigluQuantV2` and most PyTorch references; (B) **interleaved stride-2** (gpt-oss / OpenAI): `gate = x[..., ::2]`, `linear = x[..., 1::2]`, used by `swiglu_mode=1` of `aclnnDequantSwigluQuantV2`. CANN convention NOT documented in torch_npu help — must determine empirically via the fingerprint workflow above. Op#11 evidence: probe pp-1 found `α=1.702 + L=7.0 + b=1.0` matched gpt-oss exactly (`gpt_oss/torch/model.py:358-365`) → interleaved stride-2 layout → bit-exact 100% match across 8 case combinations. **AscendC implementation choices for stride-2 extraction**: (a) `DataCopyPad` with `srcStride/dstStride/repeatStride` params, OR (b) full-row load + two `Mul` ops with `BinaryRepeatParams{src1BlkStride, src1RepStride}` patterning (preferred for V220 — avoids extra MTE2), OR (c) scalar-loop GetValue (slowest, only for tiny H). **Generalizes** to any future fused activation op exposing non-trivial scalar defaults: tanh-clamp variants, GeLU-tanh-approximation parameters, RoPE base-frequency, LayerNorm-eps fingerprinting, etc.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P72](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P72** — **Dynamic int8 per-token quant tail — generic primitive chain** (domain: reduction_quant; trigger: Standard tail of dequant→activation→quant ops: per-row dynamic int8 quant. Inputs: fp32 tensor `out [N, H]`. Outputs: int8 `q [N, H]` + fp32 `quant_scales [N]` (BEFORE clamp — this is what CANN returns). **Sequence**: (1) `Abs(abs_buf, out)` per row, (2) `ReduceMax<float>(amax_scalar, abs_buf, count=H)` per row → scalar, (3) emit `quant_scales[i] = amax/127.0` BEFORE clamping (this is the returned scale; clamp is internal-only div-guard), (4) `clamped_scale = max(amax/127.0, 1e-10)` via `Maxs(scale, scale, 1e-10)` to avoid div-by-zero on all-zero rows, (5) `Muls(out, out, 1/clamped_scale)` per row (literal-first per OL-82 if precision-sensitive), (6) `Mins(out, out, 127.0)` then `Maxs(out, out, -128.0)` for symmetric saturation, (7) `Cast(q_int32, out, RoundMode::CAST_RINT)` IEEE-RNE per OL-81, (8) `Cast(q_int8, q_int32, RoundMode::CAST_NONE)` per P-P46. **ERRATA (dav_3510, 2026-08-29, OL-293)**: step (8)'s int32→int8 pair is NOT in the dav_3510 Cast dispatch table — the device-side `ASCENDC_ASSERT` guard is an empty macro under `__NPU_DEVICE__`, so the Cast silently compiles to zero instructions and the int8 output is stale UB residue (bimodal repeat fingerprint: repeat 1 near-zero, repeat 2+ garbage). The direct fp32→int8 leg is equally absent. On dav_3510 the only path INTO int8 is half→int8: use int32→half(CAST_NONE)→int8(CAST_RINT) per OL-293 (exact for [-128,127] integers, no double-rounding). **Critical contract**: `ReduceMax` is hardware-deterministic per fixed input (by-construction det). The `quant_scales` returned MUST be the pre-clamp `amax/127.0` value — the clamped variant is kernel-internal guard only. Op#10/op#11 use this exact chain bit-exact vs CANN reference. **Common bug**: returning the clamped scale instead of pre-clamp causes 1-ULP residuals on near-zero rows.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P73](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P73** — **Op-signature-as-public-formula recognition (META workflow for fused activation ops)** (domain: precision; trigger: When CANN exposes a fused op via `torch_npu.npu_<X>` and its parameter list contains scalar attributes with **non-trivial default values** (i.e., not `0.0` / `1.0` / generic), those defaults are usually the FINGERPRINT of a published public formula. **Recognition checklist**: scan op signature for: `_alpha=<irrational>` (e.g. 1.702 = SiLU/Swish-1 approximation, 1.41 = √2/sqrt(π)), `clamp_limit=<small int>` (e.g. 7.0 = gpt-oss tanh saturation, 6.0 = ReLU6), `_beta=<small float>`, `_eps=<small>` (norm-class). **Action when match suspected**: (1) WebFetch `github.com/openai/gpt-oss` / `github.com/meta-llama/llama` / `github.com/mistralai/mistral-src` / model repos for the default-value triple; (2) read those repos' formula definitions; (3) probe a3 NPU reference vs that public formula on a small test case (4-row × 128-col is enough); (4) on bit-exact match, the kernel formula is now publicly traceable + KB-codifiable. **Why this matters**: avoids 50+ formula hypotheses (op#11 kw-1 worker burned ~2h before pp-1 recognized gpt-oss fingerprint and resolved in ~30 min). Pattern applies to ALL future fused activation ops where CANN op def has non-trivial defaults.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P74](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P74** — **Multi-AIC partition-dispatch via host-precomputed segment offsets** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: Any kernel whose output is the row-concat of N independent sub-computations with host-knowable per-segment row counts. `blockDim = G` (one AIC per segment); each AIC layers P-P68 inside (constexpr static tiling + on-stack TCubeTiling). Host pybind builds `cum_out[G+1]` int32 with sentinel `cum_out[G] = total_rows` so the kernel reads `end_row = cum_out[g+1]` uniformly. Per-AIC decode: `bid = GetBlockIdx(); input_row_off / M_g` from a host flag (variable-input segments → use offsets[bid..bid+1]; uniform-input segments → use bid·m_uniform). Output side always uses `cum_out`. Determinism by-construction (each output row owned by exactly one AIC, no atomicAdd, fixed mmad order). Concrete instances: grouped/segmented matmul, MoE expert dispatch, GroupedConv, segmented attention/sparse-gather. Reference-fallback: when target torch+CANN lacks `grouped_mm`, OL-89 prose-spec extension lets `model.py` loop `torch.matmul(A_g, B[g])` per segment. **Limitation**: `blockDim=G` caps parallelism; for G ≤ AIV_count with M·N·K > ~1M, the next move is 2D dispatch (aog-kernel-optimizer territory). Validated op#2 GroupedMatmul (50/50 + 16/16 PASS bit-exact, det 50/50, 1.05× median, 0+0 iters). See OL-93 for op#2 evidence record.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P75](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P75** — **Manual TBuf pipeline with explicit `SetFlag/WaitFlag<MTE2_V>` event sync** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: TBuf-based VEC pipeline (DataCopy in → V compute → DataCopy out, looped) on V220-class chips MUST use explicit `SetFlag<HardEvent::MTE2_V>(eventId) + WaitFlag<HardEvent::MTE2_V>(eventId)` (and `V_MTE3` analogue) between stages — `PipeBarrier<PIPE_ALL>()` does NOT guarantee MTE2→V completion on TBuf and triggers silent crash 507015 (PB-21). Event IDs fetched once via `GetTPipePtr()->FetchEventID(...)` outside the loop and reused per iter. Decision rule for TBuf vs TQue lives in OL-94. Use TBuf when dataflow needs multi-buffer aliasing across phases (P-P65), persistent buffers across inner loops, or other constraints TQue's fixed depth can't express; otherwise prefer TQue auto-rotation (P-P28). Validated op#27 a3 V220 (5-iter rescue from 507015 crash); DS V4 worker session surfaced as the gap that prompted codification — weaker models default to TQue without an explicit decision table to consult. **Anti-pattern**: TBuf + `PipeBarrier<PIPE_ALL>()` (silent 507015 crash).) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P76](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P76** — **Aligned-base scratch via `Duplicate(0) + scalar SetValue` for inline VEC reductions with unaligned index offsets** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: When an inline VEC reduction (`Axpy(acc, src[off], scalar, len)` or analogous) has a runtime index offset that's not 32B-aligned (e.g. conv `op_start = max(0, ceil_div(-base, stride))` is 1/2/3 for typical kernel_size+padding combos), the natural offset-Axpy formulation triggers UB-alignment crash error 340. Workaround: build an **aligned-base tmp scratch** by `Duplicate(tmp, T(0), len_pad)` then a scalar-loop `tmp.SetValue(op, src.GetValue(op*stride + base))` for the valid range, then `Axpy(acc, tmp, scalar, len_pad)` with all-aligned bases. Cost: ~`len_pad` scalar SetValues per (ic, kp) iteration — negligible on AIV vs the alignment-crash alternative. Combine with EC-23 output over-allocation pattern (pybind allocates `[B, *, len_pad]` instead of `[B, *, len]`, narrows on return). Validated op#6 ConvStandard1d (2026-04-29) — direct-VEC 1D conv via per-(batch, out_ch) AIV core. Likely-applicable to any inline reduction where the per-iter src offset is data-dependent and can be unaligned: 1D/2D/3D conv, 1D/2D pooling with stride>1, dilated patches.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P77](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P77** — **Per-iter output via `TQue<VECOUT, depth=2>` (the TQue side of the OL-94 decision)** (domain: memory_access; trigger: The TQue counterpart to P-P75: when a per-iter loop emits via `Cast(out_ub, work_ub, ...) ; DataCopy(gm_out, out_ub, count)` and the output UB region does NOT need cross-phase buffer-liveness aliasing (P-P65) or persistent-buffer semantics, **prefer `TQue<QuePosition::VECOUT, depth=2>` over a bare `TBuf<VECCALC>`** for the output buffer. The depth=2 queue's `AllocTensor`/`EnQue`/`DeQue`/`FreeTensor` rotation provides automatic MTE3↔V sync via slot rotation: slot N+1's `AllocTensor` blocks until slot N's prior MTE3 retires, removing the need for explicit `SetFlag<HardEvent::MTE3_V>+WaitFlag<...>` flags between the per-iter `DataCopy` and the next iter's V write to the same UB region. **Anti-patterns observed when worker leaves a TBuf in this slot**: (a) `PipeBarrier<PIPE_ALL>()` at iter top — disrupts TPipe's queue scheduling, op#27 Phase D iter-5 regressed to 9/10 wrong-output runs; (b) extra `PipeBarrier<PIPE_V>` between unrelated V ops — drains the V pipe prematurely, op#27 6/10 wrong. Always rotate the output buffer through a queue first; only escalate to the manual-event-sync path (P-P75) if the TQue refactor cannot apply for structural reasons. **Cross-ref**: OL-94 (decision table TQue vs TBuf), P-P75 (TBuf manual-event side), PB-21 (PipeBarrier silent crash on V220), A-P61 (det anti-patterns; TBuf-output-race is the practitioner-side downstream of A-P61's atomicAdd-upstream). Validated op#27 27_MultiMaskAttentionAggregation a3 V220 2026-04-28.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P78](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P78** — **Row-parallel data unfolding to expose AIV parallelism upstream of single-AIC cube call** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: When a workload decomposes into "unfold input → cube GEMM → reshape output" and the cube call is single-AIC-per-tile (P-P68), the unfold stage is the parallelism floor: dispatching the unfold by `(b, g)` alone leaves 50+ AIVs idle when `B·G ≪ 56`. Pattern: dispatch `blockDim_unfold = B · G · K_total` where `K_total` is the per-`(b, g)` row count of the unfolded matrix (e.g. for conv `K_total = Cin_per_g · K_h · K_w`); decode `bid → (b, g, k_idx) → (ic, kh, kw, ...)`; each AIV builds exactly one row of `HW_pad` fp32 elements via `Duplicate(0) → fill-from-source → DataCopy` (no cross-AIV communication). Determinism by-construction (each row owned by exactly one AIV). 3-kernel pipeline shape: AIV unfold (row-parallel) + AIC cube (per-(b,g)) + AIV reshape/bias (row-parallel). Combine with EC-42 (split AIV vs AIC into separate .cpp). **Generalizes** to: 2D/3D conv via im2col, attention K/V re-assembly before flash-attn cube, group-conv unfolding, segmented-batch attention. **Anti-pattern**: dispatching the unfold by `(b, g)` only — leaves 90%+ of AIVs idle when `B·G ≪ AIV_count`. Validated op#7 ConvStandard2d ko-1 (2026-04-29): direct-VEC 0.087× → cube `(b,g)`-only unfold 0.155× → row-parallel unfold **0.705× median** (4.55× over per-(b,g), 8.10× over Opt0). Slow case `[1,32,64,64] k=7` 91.3 ms → 2.31 ms (39× speedup) when row-parallel exposes K_total=1568 to the dispatcher.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P79](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P79** — **Load-reverse trick to flip ReduceMax tie-break direction (P-P57 + stable-sort tie compatibility)** (domain: sort; trigger: When a kernel uses P-P57 SIMD ReduceMax(calcIndex=true) for top-k AND must match a reference that uses `sort(stable=True, descending=False) + mask(value < kth_value)` (stable-ascending sort + mask), the tie-break directions disagree: Ascend `ReduceMax<calcIndex>` returns the **lowest** index for ties; reference's stable-ASC sort + mask keeps the **largest-original-index** at the kth boundary. fp16/bf16 quantization creates many ties, so ours and reference pick a different SUBSET of survivors → MERE/MARE = inf at the boundary positions. **Fix**: in-place reverse the working buffer `xf[0..N_pad)` after the Cast/Adds load step (e.g., scalar-loop swap or vector-rotate idiom). Pad NEG_SENTINEL at the FRONT instead of tail. ReduceMax now returns reduced_idx; convert back to original via `orig_idx = N_pad - 1 - reduced_idx`. Mask via `xf.SetValue(reduced_idx, NEG_SENTINEL)` so the next iter picks the next-largest. The reversed-coordinate "lowest-reduced-idx" maps to "largest-original-idx" — matches reference's tie convention exactly. **Generalizes**: any P-P57 user that needs PyTorch stable-ASC compatibility. **Anti-patterns** that don't work: secondary-key sort by -orig_idx within ties (more expensive); flipping dtype sentinel (orthogonal); expanding tie-inclusion buffer K_MAX_TIE alone (only matters when k_input == K_MAX, refuted on op#9 TopKTopP a3 — tied 0/24 → 0/24 fp16 cases). Validated op#9 TopKTopP a3 V200 2026-04-30 (aog-precision-probe `topktopp-pp-1` 8-iter probe): 22/50 → 43/50 (+21 cases) with load-reverse alone, then +1 with ascending-cumsum top-p walk = 44/50. Compare to P-P60 which addresses the same tie-direction issue for the V220-only `Sort<>` API; P-P79 is the V200-portable counterpart for ReduceMax-based top-k.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P80](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P80** — **Vectorized index-compression emit via GatherMask for predicate-driven kernels** (domain: scatter_add; trigger: For kernels of the shape "emit positions where predicate is true" (e.g. nonzero, where, scatter-with-mask, sparse compress), the canonical multi-core SIMD V4 pattern is: (1) `CompareScalar` over fp32-promoted source → packed bitmask UB (one bit per element, LSB-first); (2) `ArithProgression<int32>(pos_local, 0, 1, tile_aligned)` to materialize local positions [0, tile); (3) `GatherMask<int32, uint32>(pos_compressed, pos_local, ReinterpretCast<uint32_t>(mask_packed), reduceMode=true, mask=tile_size, params={1,1,0,0}, rsvdCnt)` — compresses positions where bitmask is set into a dense prefix; `rsvdCnt` returns count by reference; (4) `Cast<int64,int32>(flat64, pos_compressed)` + `Adds<int64>(flat64, base_offset)` to convert to global flat index; (5) per N-D dim, `Divs<int64>` / `Muls<int64>` / `Sub<int64>` chunked at 32B-aligned CHUNK_ROWS=256 for UB budget; (6) scalar pack into row-major out_ub + DataCopy bulk emit to GM. **Determinism**: GatherMask emits in increasing input-position order → A-P61 fixed-order multi-core merge ALLOWED when each block owns disjoint input/output ranges. **Caveats**: (a) ALL SIMD binary-scalar ops MUST use int64 (not int32) per PB-23; (b) chunk-base addresses MUST be 32B-aligned via fixed CHUNK_ROWS stride, NOT partial-chunk size (causes runtime error 340 unaligned access); (c) dense-case GM write bandwidth dominates — for sparse (<25% true) reaches ~0.5-0.9× CANN, but for dense workloads a different design (dim-major direct stream, no UB staging) is needed. Validated op#22 22_Nonzero V4 kw-5 (2026-04-30): probe-first verified GatherMask API contract (`probes/gathermask_probe/RESULTS.md`), then V4 kernel landed 50/50 + 10/10 + det 50/50 PASS, median 0.0108×, p90 0.5319×, max 0.9487× (case 19). **Generalizes to**: any "compress positions matching a predicate" kernel — torch.where, torch.masked_select, sparse coalesce, scatter-with-mask. **Cross-ref**: PB-23 (SIMD int32 reject), PB-20 (GM write workarounds for SetValue), P-P57 (SIMD ReduceMax for top-k), determinism.md A-P61 (fixed-order multi-core merge ALLOWED).) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P81](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P81** — **Runtime-bounded loop cap for top-K-style merge / scan with constexpr K_MAX buffer** (domain: sort; trigger: When a kernel uses a `constexpr K_MAX` to size a top-K-style buffer (e.g. `TOPK_CAP = max_benchmark_k + tie_margin`), and the per-row `k` is a runtime scalar that varies across the batch, the for-loop bound for the merge/scan that fills this buffer should be `Align8(k_runtime + tie_margin)`, NOT `K_MAX`. The buffer itself stays sized at `K_MAX` (initialized once per row to a sentinel value such as `-inf`), but the work-loop terminates at the per-row bound. Snippet: `int32_t loop_cap = Align8(k_runtime + tie_margin); if (loop_cap > K_MAX) loop_cap = K_MAX; for (int32_t k = 0; k < loop_cap; ++k) { /* merge/scan */ }`. Buffer init MUST cover the full K_MAX so positions `[loop_cap..K_MAX)` read as sentinel (preserves downstream cumsum/threshold-walk invariants). Downstream copy-back uses `loop_cap` as count. **Why it works**: saves `(K_MAX − loop_cap) × ops_per_iter` of scalar-pipe work per chunk per row; for top-K-then-top-P sampling where `k` is typically 64–128 and `K_MAX` is 1088, this is an 8–15× work reduction in the merge stage. **Determinism (P-P61-class)**: `loop_cap` is a pure function of the runtime `k_runtime` scalar, identical across repeat runs; per-row path width varies but each row's path is deterministic. P-P61 4-prong (single-AIV-per-row, no atomic, hardware Sort + scalar merge, queue-rotated output) is unaffected. **Anti-pattern boundary**: if `k_runtime ≥ K_MAX − tie_margin` for ALL rows (i.e. everyone uses `k = K_MAX − tie_margin` so `loop_cap` saturates at `K_MAX`), the optimization yields zero gain. Useful only when the `k`-distribution has variance. **Generalizes** to any constexpr-capped iterative reduction whose true work is per-row variable: top-K, top-P, beam search prefix, segment-sum cap, masked nucleus. Validated op#9 9_TopKTopP ko-1 (2026-05-02 Ascend950PR_9579): scalar_ratio 0.94→0.78 (fp32 small k=64), 0.91→0.72 (fp16 mid k=128); wall-clock −75% / −70% on small/mid; bf16 large k=1024 unchanged (loop_cap saturates at K_MAX). Pass A 16/16 bit-exact + canonical det 50/50 preserved. Median ratio 0.271×→0.397× (+47%). **Cross-ref**: P-P59 (TOPK_CAP sizing for ties — sets the K_MAX), P-P60 (Sort tie direction), P-P79 (load-reverse for ReduceMax-based top-k tie).) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P82](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P82** — Deterministic cross-AIV tournament merge of P partial top-K buffers via composite key (value DESC, orig_idx ASC) (domain: sort; trigger: multi-AIV top-K merge needing PyTorch-stable tie semantics) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P83](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P83** — **Column-major intermediate output + pybind transpose to eliminate per-element scalar pack in predicate-driven N-D coord emit** (domain: scatter_add; trigger: When the V4 GatherMask emit pattern (P-P80) is bottlenecked on the per-element scalar pack from column-major coord UB → row-major output GM (per-element interleave at ~10–15 scalar cycles per int64 dimension), the **column-major intermediate output composite** eliminates the scalar pack entirely. **Kernel side**: allocate output GM as `[ndim, numel]` column-major instead of `[numel, ndim]` row-major; after SIMD coord decode produces `coord64[d * CHUNK_ROWS + r]` per dim, emit each dim's contiguous chunk via aligned `DataCopy(outputGm[d * numel + my_offset + emitted], coord64[d * CHUNK_ROWS], chunk_aligned)` (chunk_aligned = `(chunk/4)*4` for int64 32B alignment; tail <4 elements via uint32 split-AtomicAdd on pre-zeroed slots). No `out_ub` row-major staging buffer needed — saves UB and eliminates per-element scalar copy. **Pybind side**: allocate `torch::empty({ndim, numel}, kInt64, kNPU)`, kernel writes column-major, then `output_cm.slice(1, 0, K).transpose(0, 1).contiguous()` returns `[K, ndim]` row-major to caller. Pybind transpose is a single GM→GM strided copy of `K * ndim * 8` bytes by torch (~400 GB/s HBM → ~2.5 ms for 67M-row dense). **Determinism (A-P61)**: each block writes disjoint `[d * numel + my_offset, ... + my_count)` per dim — no race; within block, GatherMask emits in increasing pos order, SIMD decode is order-fixed, DataCopy is bulk MTE3 deterministic; pybind transpose deterministic per torch contract. **When to use**: predicate-driven emit kernels with N-D coord decode (N > 1) where the V4 P-P80 pattern is profile-confirmed bottlenecked on the scalar interleave. **When NOT useful**: ndim==1 (no interleave to avoid); kernel-natural layout already matches output contract (rare for index ops); stretch perf demand requires fused single-pass transpose-fused kernel (different architecture). Validated op#22 22_Nonzero V5 kw-2 (2026-05-03 Ascend950PR_9579): Pass A 50/50 + Pass B 10/10 bit-exact, Det 50/50, perf overall 0.3563× (median 0.2832×) vs V4 baseline 0.1603× (median 0.0368×) → **2.22× cumulative speedup**, 7.7× median improvement. Some sparse cases now beat CANN (max 1.79×). **Generalizes** to any N-D index-emit kernel sharing the column-major-coord-UB → row-major-GM scalar-pack bottleneck (where, masked_select, sparse coalesce, scatter-with-mask, sparse-COO build). **Cross-ref**: P-P80 (the V4 pattern this composite improves on — same kernel surface), PB-23 (SIMD int32 reject — int64 indices required throughout), determinism.md A-P61 (block-disjoint write ordering preserved).) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P85](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P85** — **`AscendC::TopK` adv_api primitive — use this instead of hand-rolled chunked-Sort + scalar 2-pointer merge for k-selection on arch 3510/5102/3003/3113** (domain: sort; trigger: When implementing TopK / TopKTopP / quantile / threshold-mask kernels on Ascend950PR (arch 3510) and similar, the public `AscendC::TopK<T, isInitIndex, isHasfinish, isReuseSrc, topkMode, config>` from `adv_api/topk/topk.h` (host helpers `GetTopKMaxMinTmpSize` + `TopKTilingFunc` from `adv_api/sort/topk_tiling.h`) delegates to vec-pipe-bound primitives (likely the private `KernelVbsMergeSort` family in `opp/built-in/op_impl/.../arch35/merge_sort_simd.h`). **dtype matrix** — `TopKConfig::algo`: `RADIX_SELECT` supports ALL int + half + float + **bf16**; `MERGE_SORT` supports half + float ONLY (no bf16). For bf16 hot-path ops RADIX_SELECT is mandatory; for fp16/fp32 benchmark both. **Snippet**: `TopKConfig cfg{ TopKAlgo::RADIX_SELECT, TopKOrder::LARGEST, /*sorted=*/true }; TopKTilingFunc(platform, /*inner=*/N, /*outter=*/B_per_AIV, k_runtime, sizeof(T), false, TopKMode::TOPK_NORMAL, true, cfg, tiling);` host-side, then `AscendC::TopK<T, false, false, false, TopKMode::TOPK_NORMAL, cfg>(sortedVal, sortedIdx, srcVal, dummyIdx, finishLocal, tmpLocal, k_runtime, tiling, info, true);` kernel-side. **Anti-pattern (current op#9 9_TopKTopP Phase 1 pre-kw-4)**: chunked `AscendC::Sort<>` over CHUNK_LEN=2048 per chunk + scalar 2-pointer merge into TOPK_CAP buffer → `aiv_scalar_ratio=0.898` (scalar-pipe bound) → 1024 us/row for [N=65536, k=1024] bf16. **CANN's own `npu_top_k_top_p` internal Sort kernel** (measured via msprof on a single fused call): `aiv_vec_ratio=0.721`, 204 us/row for the same shape — **5× faster**, vec-pipe-bound, almost certainly using the adv_api or equivalent vec-merge primitive. **Determinism risk (medium)**: RADIX_SELECT may have its own tie-break ordering — characterize with a 5-rep det-check before adopting on DET_POLICY=required ops. **Generalizes** to any kernel currently doing chunked-Sort + scalar-merge for k-selection: rejection-sampling top-K, beam-search prefix, threshold-mask + scatter, sparse-select. **Cross-ref**: P-P81 (runtime-bounded loop cap — orthogonal optimization once the merge primitive is replaced); EC-33 (RADIX_SORT in `Sort<>` chunked context still defensive-MERGE per EC-33; AscendC::TopK::RADIX_SELECT is a DIFFERENT API path from `Sort<>::RADIX_SORT` — does NOT trip EC-33 per pp-2 measurement); P-P84 (analytical decomposition methodology — anti-pattern fixed by P-P85's "use the adv_api primitive" approach). Validated empirically op#9 9_TopKTopP pp-2 (2026-05-03 Ascend950PR_9579, CANN 9.0.0 b103) — measured 3.35× total gap is recoverable, NOT a structural ceiling. kw-4 spawn pending.) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P86](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P86** — **Empirical-measurement-required for fused-op gap analysis — analytical-only decomposition is reward-hacking on the diagnostic side** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: When estimating "CANN's per-sub-op cost" for a fused op, analytical-only decomposition (estimate "CANN's Phase-X equivalent" by selecting a standalone CANN op that LOOKS LIKE Phase X, then compute analytical gap) is unreliable AND can be wildly wrong. **Op#9 9_TopKTopP fo-1 vs pp-2 evidence (2026-05-03 Ascend950PR_9579)**: fo-1 estimated `npu_top_k(k=1024)` standalone at ~7 us/row → claimed "146× gap on Phase 1" → declared structural ceiling. pp-2 directly msprof'd `torch_npu.npu_top_k_top_p` and found CANN's internal Sort kernel runs at **204 us/row** (full-row sort, not k=1024-only). Real gap = **5× on Phase 1, 3.35× overall** — **fo-1's claim was off by ~30×**. The analytical estimate was wrong because fused ops decompose internally into sub-ops with DIFFERENT shapes than the user-facing standalone equivalents (CANN's `npu_top_k_top_p` does full-row sort then apply-on-sorted; standalone `npu_top_k(k=K)` does only top-K — different cost model). **Methodology rule (mandatory for fused-op gap analysis)**: profile BOTH (1) `torch_npu.<fused_op>(...)` directly with msprof to observe internal kernel decomposition (kernel names, BlockDim, dur, pipe ratios per internal kernel), AND (2) each kernel name from (1) — that's the actual sub-op-level reference cost. If only fused-op msprof is available, per-row dur is still tighter than analytical estimates: divide by BlockDim × per-AIV row count. **Sustained-call EC-33 mitigation for the standalone-ref measurement step**: each measurement should run in a FRESH Python process (multi-process msprof), not back-to-back in one process — pp-2 measured this directly (M2 process aborted after 3 sub-ops succeeded clean within one process; M1+M1b in fresh processes were stable). **Block conditions (when this rule applies)**: any time aog-fused-optimizer / aog-kernel-optimizer / orchestrator is about to declare "structural ceiling" or "perf plateau" on a fused op. Analytical-only verdict is INSUFFICIENT — must include measured per-internal-kernel msprof of the fused reference op. **Generalizes**: any multi-stage fused op where the user-facing standalone equivalents may use different internal algorithms (Softmax+Mul, RmsNorm+Cast, RoPE+Cache, etc.). Validated op#9 fo-1 vs pp-2 cross-comparison 2026-05-03. **Cross-ref**: P-P84 (the analytical methodology — now downgraded as fallback-only when measurement is impossible); aog-self-critic C27 + new C28 (this rule is C28's empirical-evidence requirement).) | L1 |
| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P84](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P84** — **Sub-op gap decomposition via msprof PipeUtilization anchor + analytical scalar-op counting (fused-optimizer methodology when standalone refs are unreliable)** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: For multi-stage fused ops where running per-sub-op standalone CANN refs (`npu_top_k`, `npu_softmax`, `npu_cumsum`, etc.) is unreliable due to EC-33-class instability, NPU lane contention, or shared-resource concerns, decompose per-row dur into per-sub-op contributions analytically. **Procedure**: (1) take ONE high-quality msprof PipeUtilization profile of the dominant case — record `aiv_scl_ratio`, `aiv_vec_ratio`, `aiv_mte2_ratio`, `aiv_mte3_ratio`, total dur, rows-per-AIV; (2) per-row dur = total_dur / rows_per_aiv; per-pipe budget = pipe_ratio × per_row_dur; (3) for each sub-op, count scalar ops (GetValue/SetValue/Set+Get pairs) by code inspection — calibrate per-op latency from observed dur (typically 3–6 ns/op on Ascend950PR scalar pipe); (4) per-sub-op scalar contribution = scalar_op_count × calibrated_latency; vector contribution via cycle accounting (e.g. ReduceMax over N ≈ N/64 + log₂(64)); (5) sub-op classification: BOTTLENECK if contribution > 50% of dominant pipe budget; UNCOMPARABLE if no standalone ref exists for the sub-op's full role (e.g. row-max into shared accumulator across phases); NECESSARY if structurally required (e.g. emit phase doing full-row scatter). **When applicable**: fused op where `npu_<X>` standalone ref exists but cannot be measured cleanly; per-AIV serial work dominates (single-AIV-per-row); bottleneck dominated by scalar pipe (analytical counting gives high-confidence cost). **Limitations**: ±2× per-cell precision (qualitatively decisive for finding dominant cell, not for ranking near-equal cells); doesn't help when standalone refs ARE reliable — direct measurement is better. **Anti-pattern complement**: When the localized bottleneck is scalar-pipe-bound (`aiv_scl_ratio > 0.6`), buffer-aliasing optimizations have ~zero ROI even if the aliasing is correct — recovered UB doesn't help unless it unlocks a tile/chunk-size increase that itself unlocks vector work. Op#9 fo-1 found 19.4 KB recoverable from idle multi-AIV buffers + 8.8 KB from sortedVal/Idx ↔ mergeTmp aliasing — ~28 KB total. CHUNK_LEN expansion 2048→4096 would consume 16 KB more (fits!), but archive iter 2 already tried this and reverted for **precision** (Sort intrinsic drift on >2048 elements), independent of UB. So even with the alias fix, the obvious downstream lever isn't safe. Validated op#9 9_TopKTopP fo-1 (2026-05-03 Ascend950PR_9579): localized 81% of dur to Phase 1 inner loop (32 chunks × 1088 merge_cap × 6 scalar ops × ~5ns ≈ 740 us/row of 1264 us/row total). Saved 4× standalone-CANN benchmark calls that would have hit EC-33 truncation. Verdict: structural ceiling 0.385× confirmed via 3rd independent diagnostic angle. **Cross-ref**: MSPROF_AGENT_GUIDE.md (PipeUtilization extraction), EC-33 (sustained-call instability rationale for analytical decomposition), OL-82 (scalar_ratio thresholds).) | L1 |

| [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P99](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P99** — **Manual-Mmad B-operand contraction axis = the L1 tile's C0/inner dim; A and B must source the contraction from the SAME axis** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: hand-rolled tile-MMAD `Mmad`+`LoadData` L1→L0 loads. 2D `LoadNzL1ToZnL0B` contracts over the L1 C0 dim; A and B must both contract over C0 (plain loads) OR both over ROW (matched transposed/3D loads). | [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P100](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P100** — **Hand-rolled Mmad: output-n and contraction-k axes spanning >1 C0 block (>BASE_K=16) MUST be tiled in BASE_K chunks (+accumulate for k)** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: a single `LoadData`+`Mmad` with n or k > one C0 block mis-packs fractals (alternating-zero output columns for n / mis-contract for k). Tile n in BASE_K col-blocks (Fixpipe to `out[..,ni*BASE_K]`); tile k in BASE_K with `cmatrixInitVal=(ki==0)` 4-arg accumulate-Mmad. Reduces to single-shot when axis ≤16, so D=16 unaffected. | [patterns/domains/a3_mix_small_matmul_cube.md](target/ascendc/patterns/domains/a3_mix_small_matmul_cube.md) | **P-P120** — **Neumann / (I+P) triangular-solve L0C-fold via accumulate-mode Mmad (`cmatrixInitVal`) on a3 (Ascend910_9382, arch22): compute `R@(I+P)=R+R@P` in ONE L0C pass, NO separate +I vector-add** — for an iterative (I+P) matmul chain (Neumann power-product `A=(I+x)^-1=Π_i(I+x^{2^i})`, each step `R_next=R@(I+P)`) inverting `(I+strict-lower)` in chunked gated-delta-rule / gated-linear-attention. First Mmad `cmatrixInitVal=1` seeds the L0C accumulator with the identity/additive term (`R`), second Mmad `cmatrixInitVal=0` accumulates `R@P` onto the SAME L0C → `R+R@P=R@(I+P)`; hold one L0C accumulator across the chain, Fixpipe only at the end. Same accumulate idiom as the FA K-tile loop (`matmul_primitive`), repurposed to inject an ALGEBRAIC additive term. **Measured**: cuts AIV ops + barriers to ~6 vs ~9 for a 5-step chain; precision unchanged (additive term exact in fp32 L0C — more faithful than fp16 round-tripped vector add). Watch the solve SIGN (CAND-GDR-1) — fold is sign-agnostic, orthogonal. Reference STRUCTURE cv-ref `NeumannSolve`. | [patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md#P-P87](target/ascendc/patterns/PATTERN_INDEX.md) | **P-P87** — **Vendor adv_api regbase primitive substitution requires per-call batch axis A>1 to amortize internal scalar-broadcast cost** (domain: platform_compat; trigger: When considering substituting hand-rolled per-row code with a vendor `AscendC::<Primitive><U,T>` adv_api call (Normalize, LayerNorm, RowMuls, Softmax, Logit, etc.), the perf advantage materializes ONLY when the dispatch shape supplies multiple rows (A>1) per primitive call. Vendor c310 regbase impls (e.g. `normalize_c310_impl.h`) use `Reg::LoadAlign<DIST_BRC_B32>` to broadcast scalar inputs (mean, rstd, gamma, beta, scale, etc.) into vector registers across A rows in ONE call — the broadcast-load cost is fixed per call, so per-row cost scales as `(broadcast_cost / A) + per_row_compute`. At A=1 the broadcast happens once per row → no amortization → vendor primitive performs same as (or worse than) hand-rolled code. **Decision rule (mandatory pre-substitution check)**: (a) for **pure precision improvement** (e.g. CPU-truth alignment via vendor's bit-canonical regbase output), A=1 substitution is net-positive — the regbase impl produces bit-identical results and never regresses precision; (b) for **perf improvement**, FIRST verify the call-site dispatch can supply A>1. If outer loop is per-row (A=1, each `Normalize` call processes 1 row of K elements), the substitution is NOT a perf lever — needs upstream restructure (Kind-2 batch rewrite: load K rows into one buffer of shape [A, K], single batched call, scatter results back). **Snippet**: vendor signature is typically `<Primitive><U,T>(dst[A][K], src[A][K], scalars[A], ...)` — the leading A axis IS the amortization axis; never call with A=1 expecting perf gain. **Anti-pattern (op#10 LayerNorm kw-2-this-session 2026-05-05)**: substituted `AscendC::Normalize<{half\) | L3 |

Distinctive signature: **every tile/chunk wrong EXCEPT the last** (last has no successor reload). Cross-ITERATION analogue of PB-17 (which is the cross-ROW alias variant within `ProcessRow`). Fix: `if(l0>0) SyncVtoMTE2();` (`SetFlag`/`WaitFlag<HardEvent::V_MTE2>`) at chunk-loop top (~8 lines). Distinct from PB-47 (PB-47 = AscendC SIMD/AIV UB V→MTE2 fence; PB-48 = SIMT GM cross-iteration, fix is NOT a fence). Fix: don't stage the carry through GM — keep it all-UB/registers OR recompute the carry from the chunk boundary each iteration. Anchor: selective_scan coop-bwd L-chunk 2026-06-23 A5/Ascend950PR_957b CANN 9.1.T500 — forward-x staged to GM scratch read stale across L-chunks, `asc_threadfence` did not fix; all-UB/recompute-from-boundary cleared it (whitebox). Status OPEN (architectural SIMT coherency, not CANN). Cross-ref PB-47 (SIMD/AIV UB sibling), P-P106, LANGUAGE_REFERENCE `asc_threadfence`. backend=all. | L3 (SIMT GM cross-iteration coherency) |

| [OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md#OL-245](target/ascendc/OPERATIONAL_KNOWLEDGE.md) | **OL-245** — **On A5/arch35, SIMD compute CHAINS default to REGBASE, NOT Membase/LocalTensor** — Membase (LocalTensor `Add`/`Mul`/`Cast`) round-trips every intermediate through UB (store+reload + a `PipeBarrier<PIPE_V>` per dependent op) → ~N redundant UB round-trips + N barriers for an N-op chain = a major vector-utilization-gap source. Regbase (MicroAPI `__VEC_SCOPE__` + `RegTensor<float>` + `LoadAlign` head / reg→reg `Mul`/`Add` / `StoreAlign` tail) loads UB→reg ONCE, keeps the whole chain in registers, stores ONCE — round-trips + per-step barriers vanish. Membase OK only for trivial single-op / bitwise cases. **Anti-pattern (why it's an OL)**: regbase was SILOED in FA-class wholeport templates + `migration/` guides; the general SIMD-gen path (SIMD_DEVELOPMENT_REFERENCE + kw_brief general recipe) was Membase-only → kw never surfaced regbase for non-FA SIMD ops. Evidence: selective_scan SIMD fwd+bwd were 100% Membase → ~15× redundant vector work, ~33× off roofline floor (2490µs vs ~75µs); expert input 2026-06-23. **BOUNDARY (perf-loop iter-2 2026-06-23)**: regbase wins require LARGE per-VF-call amortization — the `__VEC_SCOPE__` per-call entry/exit overhead ≈0.42µs/call; an isolated 8-op fp32 chain is 1.26× FASTER with regbase but a high-frequency small-shallow-tile op (selective_scan fwd build, ~30720 calls on 64-tile 3-op chunks) is 1.27× SLOWER → MEASURE before regbase-rewriting a fine-grained op, don't blind-rewrite.
